# Project AMANOR - Agent Delivery Plan

> Living execution ledger for the Project AMANOR website. This file is the source of truth for scope, ownership, dependencies, status, verification, blockers, and handoffs.

| Field                   | Value                                                                                                   |
| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Project                 | Project AMANOR - The Authority Platform                                                                 |
| Project key             | `AMANOR`                                                                                                |
| Source specification    | `Project_AMANOR_Website_Build_Brief.pdf`, version 1.0, 26 July 2026                                     |
| Operating guidance      | `AI_Development_Workflow_Training_Manual.docx`; `AI_Native_Software_Engineering_Operations_Manual.docx` |
| Plan created            | 9 August 2026                                                                                           |
| Current lifecycle phase | Architecture revision / Sprint 0 foundation                                                             |
| Target public launch    | 16 weeks after approved Phase 0 kickoff                                                                 |
| Delivery cadence        | Two-week sprints; working software reviewed with the Principal every fortnight                          |
| Status authority        | This ledger, reconciled with Jira, GitHub, CI, deployed environments, and approval evidence             |

### Status vocabulary

| Status        | Meaning                                                                                        |
| ------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `BACKLOG`     | Defined but not ready to start.                                                                |
| `BLOCKED`     | Cannot progress until the named dependency or decision is resolved.                            |
| `READY`       | Acceptance criteria and dependencies are sufficient for an owner to claim.                     |
| `IN PROGRESS` | Claimed by exactly one owner and actively being implemented.                                   |
| `IN REVIEW`   | Implementation complete; review or QA remains.                                                 |
| `QA`          | In functional, integration, regression, accessibility, security, or performance testing.       |
| `UAT`         | Awaiting or undergoing client acceptance.                                                      |
| `DONE`        | Code/content complete, tests and approvals passed, merged, documentation and trackers updated. |
| `DEFERRED`    | Intentionally removed from the current launch scope with approval.                             |

### Truth rules

- A document or task is not `DONE` because it has been discussed or scaffolded.
- Every `IN PROGRESS` item must name one owner, branch, start date, and reserved paths.
- Every `DONE` item must link or name its verification evidence and approval where applicable.
- Feature status is the lowest status of its required launch stories, not an optimistic average.
- External approvals, content, legal review, security review, and real-device checks stay explicit.
- Jira epics use feature codes `F01`-`F15`; page stories include `P01`-`P13` in their summaries.
- Branches: `feature/AMANOR-<id>-<slug>`; commits and pull requests begin with `AMANOR-<id>`.
- Short-lived branches, one approving review, and a green pipeline are required before merge.
- Agents must preserve unrelated work, record handoffs here, and avoid editing another owner's reserved paths.

## 2. Confirmed decisions and architecture baseline

These decisions supersede conflicting stack choices in Section 10.1 of the build brief. Product, compliance, performance, localisation, security, and acceptance requirements remain in force.

| Decision             | Confirmed choice                                                             | Status               | Consequence / follow-up                                                                                                                          |
| -------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Public frontend      | Next.js 16, App Router, strict TypeScript in `apps/web`                      | `CONFIRMED`          | Public application is independently built and deployed; it has no direct MongoDB or Mongoose access.                                             |
| Admin/CMS frontend   | Separate Next.js 16 application in `apps/admin`                              | `CONFIRMED`          | Admin and CMS are one protected operator application, independently built and deployed from the public site and API.                             |
| Backend              | NestJS API in `apps/api`                                                     | `CONFIRMED`          | All business logic, MongoDB/Mongoose access, authentication, CMS workflows, audit, integrations, jobs and OpenAPI contracts live behind the API. |
| CMS                  | First-party custom CMS, with UI in `apps/admin` and domain/API in `apps/api` | `CONFIRMED`          | Drafts, versioning, localisation, approval, publishing and audit are backend-owned; admin is only an authorised client.                          |
| Operational database | MongoDB                                                                      | `CONFIRMED`          | Define bounded collections, transactions, indexes, encryption boundaries, backups, and forward-only migrations.                                  |
| ODM                  | Mongoose                                                                     | `CONFIRMED`          | Use explicit schemas, runtime validation, indexes, migrations, and repository boundaries.                                                        |
| Media                | Cloudinary                                                                   | `CONFIRMED`          | Images originate as local files uploaded through governed signed Admin workflows. Content/profile records select registry asset IDs and never accept an image URL; delivery uses responsive transforms, strict metadata and budgets. |
| Admin authentication | Custom JWT authentication issued and enforced by NestJS                      | `CONFIRMED`          | Admin uses short-lived access JWTs and backend-hosted rotating refresh cookies; the public app never receives admin credentials.                 |
| Maps                 | Leaflet                                                                      | `CONFIRMED`          | Keep Atlas accessible table twin, lazy loading, low-bandwidth fallback, and tile-provider degradation plan.                                      |
| Styling              | Tailwind CSS with bespoke design tokens; no public component library         | `ADOPTED FROM BRIEF` | Admin-only component primitives are allowed if documented.                                                                                       |
| Localisation         | `en-GB` and `fr-FR` at parity                                                | `ADOPTED FROM BRIEF` | Store locales together with `current`, `stale`, or `missing` per-field status.                                                                   |
| Rendering            | Static/server rendering in `apps/web`; dynamic APIs in `apps/api`            | `ADOPTED FROM BRIEF` | NestJS publish events call a signed web revalidation endpoint; content, public web, admin and API release independently.                         |
| Email                | Resend on authenticated sending subdomain                                    | `PROPOSED`           | Confirm provider; SPF, DKIM, DMARC and institutional deliverability are launch gates.                                                            |
| Hosting / edge       | Vercel plus Cloudflare WAF/cache/bot management                              | `PROPOSED`           | Confirm accounts, regions, preview protection, secrets, and rollback design.                                                                     |
| Analytics            | Self-hosted Plausible                                                        | `PROPOSED`           | Confirm privacy/legal position and hosting.                                                                                                      |
| Observability        | Prometheus/Grafana, Loki, Opsgenie                                           | `PROPOSED`           | Confirm operational ownership and hosting model.                                                                                                 |
| AI for F05           | Retrieval-grounded model behind internal server API                          | `BLOCKED`            | Principal must choose Doctrine Engine vs curated Positions page; provider and MongoDB vector-search design need an ADR.                          |
| PDF generation       | Headless browser with dedicated print routes/styles                          | `ADOPTED FROM BRIEF` | Protect generation endpoints and test bilingual print output.                                                                                    |

### Deployment topology - confirmed 9 August 2026

```text
Browser
  |
  +--> www.ishmaeldodoo.*  -> apps/web   (Next.js 16 public frontend)
  +--> admin.ishmaeldodoo.* -> apps/admin (Next.js 16 protected CMS/admin)
  +--> api.ishmaeldodoo.*   -> apps/api   (NestJS backend)

apps/web   -- public/server content reads --> apps/api
apps/admin -- credentialed admin requests --> apps/api
apps/api   -- Mongoose only ---------------> MongoDB
apps/api   -- signed media operations -----> Cloudinary
apps/api   -- signed publish webhook ------> apps/web revalidation endpoint
```

Boundary rules:

- `apps/web`, `apps/admin`, and `apps/api` have separate build commands, environment schemas, health checks, deployment manifests, release versions, logs and rollback paths.
- Only `apps/api` may import Mongoose, connect to MongoDB, issue/verify admin JWTs, hold Cloudinary/Resend/calendar secrets, or execute business workflows.
- `apps/admin` never imports backend repositories or models. It consumes generated API contracts and keeps access tokens in memory; refresh cookies are host-only on the API origin.
- `apps/web` consumes published, cache-safe API projections. It cannot read drafts or privileged endpoints and contains no database credentials.
- Shared packages contain contracts, generated clients, lint/TypeScript configuration and safe UI primitives only. They contain no secrets, database adapters or server runtime state.
- Production origins should share the same registrable parent domain. NestJS CORS is an exact allow-list, credentialed only for the admin origin; CSRF and Origin enforcement remain mandatory.

## 12. Quality engineering and acceptance

### Per-story Definition of Done

- Meets the approved specification and acceptance criteria in both locales where applicable.
- Verified responsively at 360, 768, 1024, and 1440 pixels.
- Keyboard-operable with visible focus; axe-core clean; interactive features manually screen-reader tested.
- Works in Sahel Mode and respects reduced-motion, contrast, text scaling, and touch-target requirements.
- Meets CI performance budgets: LCP <= 1.8 s, INP <= 150 ms, CLS <= 0.05, initial JS <= 120 KB except Atlas, standard page <= 500 KB, Sahel path <= 200 KB, Atlas TTI <= 2.5 s on 4G, Lighthouse accessibility 100.
- Logic has unit tests; data/integration boundaries have integration tests; user journeys have Playwright coverage.
- No hard-coded public copy or identity/title strings; data resolves from the CMS/registry.
- Security, privacy, logging, localisation and failure/degradation states are tested.
- One engineer approves the PR; Product Lead accepts against the brief; merge, Jira and this ledger are updated.

| AMANOR-051/067 audience reset | Claude | `RELEASED` | `main` (initial local repository; no remote) | Possession taken of `apps/web/src/components/content/audience-doors.tsx` and **released with the file byte-identical to how it was found.** | 10 Aug 2026 | 10 Aug 2026 - **investigated, attempted fix reverted, diagnosis handed to the AMANOR-051/067 owner.** The journey fails on `expect(localStorage.getItem("amanor-audience")).toBeNull()` receiving `"investor"` after Reset view: the cookie and the rendered view reset while the stored preference survives, so a reset a visitor performs for privacy reasons does not reset. It is **intermittent, not deterministic** - Firefox passed one run and failed the next before any change of mine, and Chromium and WebKit both flip. `audience-doors.test.tsx` passes because `writePreference(null)` is correct in isolation, so the defect is in the navigation path, not the helper. Most likely cause: the mount effect `useEffect(() => writePreference(selected), [selected])` re-persists whatever `selected` the server sends, so any race in which `/api/audience` has not yet cleared the cookie before the redirect renders writes the stale audience straight back. Making the control a real navigation instead of a client transition did **not** reliably fix it, so that change was reverted rather than left unverified in another owner's file. Suggested direction: stop the mount effect writing storage as a side effect of rendering, or make `/api/audience` prove the cookie is cleared before redirecting. |

Focused Web tests pass 17/17, the full public matrix passes 42/42 across Chromium/Firefox/WebKit and the complete root gate passes. Provider/privacy approval and production QA remain external. |

| AMANOR-125/136 shared fixture lock | Claude | `IN REVIEW` | `main` (initial local repository; no remote) | **Taking possession from the AMANOR-136 and AMANOR-125 owners:** `scripts/run-e2e.mjs` and `scripts/verify-mongo-backup-restore.mjs` (fixture locking only; key generation, TLS, build, backup and restore logic are untouched), plus the new `scripts/lib/fixture-lock.mjs`. | 11 Aug 2026 | 11 Aug 2026 - a second browser run started while a first was live tore down the shared compose fixture and killed the first mid-suite. The runner now takes and releases a PID lock so the second run is refused with a clear message, stale locks are reclaimed, and the failure can no longer masquerade as a product defect. A second robustness gap surfaced while proving this: a run killed before its teardown leaves the compose fixture up with the Room user already created, and the non-idempotent `createUser` then aborted the whole suite with `User "amanor_room_e2e@amanor_room_e2e" already exists` - again a confusing failure unrelated to the code under test. The runner now tears the fixture down before bringing it up, which the lock makes safe. A third and wider case then appeared: `scripts/verify-mongo-backup-restore.mjs` drives the **same** compose project, container and host port and calls `down` at its start, so `npm run test:backup-restore` destroys a browser suite already in flight. The lock is therefore no longer private to the browser runner: it lives in `scripts/lib/fixture-lock.mjs` and both tools take it, so whichever starts second is refused by name instead of silently corrupting the first. |

Those files are reserved by another owner, so the entry point is a handoff rather than an oversight. Add the bilingual link from P13 and include the route in the sitemap/`llms.txt` projections | `RESOLVED` |
| Two public-platform browser journeys fail on all three engines | AMANOR-035/130 analytics consent, AMANOR-051/067 adaptive audience, AMANOR-136 journey suite | Engineering (those task owners) | **Resolved 10 Aug 2026.** Audience reset previously raced a render-time storage write; destination action markers now converge cookie, storage, URL and rendered selection. Analytics appeared to lose its French state because automatic Sahel detection disabled analytics after stale speculative requests invoked cookie-mutating preference routes. Server-rendered dismissal precedence, pre-hydration-disabled consent controls, read-only RSC/prefetch handling and `prefetch={false}` on every preference action remove those races. The two disputed journeys pass 6/6 across Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; the complete public-platform suite passes 42/42 and the root gate passes. **Both halves fixed by the AMANOR-035/051 owner after this diagnosis was handed over, and independently re-verified here on 11 Aug 2026: the two journeys now pass 6/6 across Chromium, Firefox and WebKit.** The audience mount effect that re-persisted server state was removed, and the analytics consent path was corrected alongside `layout.tsx` and `proxy.ts`. | `RESOLVED` |

| Atlas P03 responsiveness journey fails on all three engines | AMANOR-053/068 Atlas, AMANOR-136 journey suite | Engineering (those task owners) | `e2e/atlas-evidence.spec.ts:4` asserts `getByRole("row")` has count 5 and receives 10 on Chromium, Firefox and WebKit. Diagnosed as a **stale expectation, not a rendering defect**: the accessible table emits one header row plus nine distinct data rows, and the captured cells hold different values rather than repeats, so the E2E seed has grown past the four-node fixture the assertion was written for. The sibling print journey passes. **Fixed by Claude on 11 Aug 2026 after taking possession of `e2e/atlas-evidence.spec.ts`.** The journey no longer asserts a literal row count. It identifies the four seeded acts by their role cells, then asserts the row count is above four and **identical at 360 and 1440** - which is what a responsiveness journey should actually prove, and cannot drift when the fixture grows. Passes 4/4 across Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, with the two print cases skipping off Chromium by design | `RESOLVED` |

| Two agents cannot run the browser suite at the same time | AMANOR-136 journey suite, any concurrent agent | Engineering | `scripts/run-e2e.mjs` drives the fixed compose project in `infra/docker-compose.test.yml` and tears it down with `down --volumes` in its `finally`. When a second run starts while a first is active, the finishing run destroys the database the other is still using, and the victim dies with `MongoWriteConcernError: Replication is being shut down` or a web server that cannot boot. Observed directly on 11 Aug 2026 with two agents in the same worktree. The failure looks like a product defect and is not one, which is the expensive part. Give the runner a per-invocation compose project name, or make it refuse to start while another run holds the fixture **Fixed by Claude on 11 Aug 2026 after taking possession of `scripts/run-e2e.mjs`.** The runner now takes a PID lock at `tmp/e2e-run.lock` before touching the fixture and releases it on every exit path, so a concurrent run is refused immediately with a message naming the holder instead of silently corrupting the first run. A holder that died without cleanup is reclaimed rather than blocking forever, and `EPERM` from `process.kill(pid, 0)` counts as alive because it means the process exists under another user. Verified both directions. | `RESOLVED` |

| Concurrent coverage runs collide on a shared per-app temp directory | AMANOR-134 coverage baseline, any concurrent agent | Engineering | **Resolved 11 Aug 2026.** Every app's coverage command now enters a shared runner that atomically creates a mode-0600 per-workspace lock before Vitest can clean or write `coverage/.tmp`. A competing process is refused with the live holder's name; duplicate same-process acquisition also fails; dead or malformed holders are reclaimed; token-bound cleanup cannot remove a replacement holder. The generic primitive also strengthens the existing Mongo fixture lock. The canonical root gate executes deterministic live-holder, duplicate-holder and stale-holder fixtures before all 618 tests and builds **Fixed by the AMANOR-134 owner after this diagnosis was filed, and independently re-verified here on 11 Aug 2026.** Each app now runs coverage through `scripts/run-coverage.mjs`, which takes a per-app advisory lock, and `check:concurrency-guards` is wired into the root gate so a workspace cannot quietly drop back to an unguarded runner. Verified directly: a live holder is refused by name, a stale holder is reclaimed, and the guard check passes. | `RESOLVED` |

| Production environment files were committable and unscanned | AMANOR-120 secret custody, AMANOR-123 secret scanning | Engineering | Writing real `apps/*/.env.production` files exposed a live leak path: `.gitignore` enumerated `.env`, `.env.local` and `.env.*.local`, so `.env.production` was **not** ignored, and `check-secrets` skipped it because `extname(".env.production")` is `".production"`, which is not a scannable extension. A production file holding an ES256 signing key was therefore both committable and invisible to the gate. Closed on 11 Aug 2026: `.gitignore` now ignores `.env.*` with `!.env.example`, and the scanner scans any file whose name begins with `.env` regardless of extension. A third defect surfaced while proving it - the scanner passed `--no-index` to `git check-ignore`, so a file force-added to the index was reported as ignored and skipped even though git would commit it; that flag is gone. Verified both directions: a tracked `.env.staging` carrying a key is reported, an untracked ignored `.env.production` is not | `RESOLVED` |

## 18. Immediate next actions

1. Principal/Product Lead reviews and approves this plan as the backlog baseline.
2. Resolve D01-D11 and S01-S07 in the controlled Phase 0 workshop. Prioritise D11 first because every standard public route currently misses the original 120 KiB initial-script ceiling; then D01, D07, D08 and D09 for launch-critical domain and Protocol Desk operation, followed by S01-S06 for deployable staging evidence. No measured baseline is approval.
3. Principal/Content approves production identity, title history, Speaking themes, Archive inventory, sources, page copy and media so implemented surfaces can advance from review.
4. Select and approve the calendar provider for AMANOR-087, execute its implemented retry/reconciliation outage drill, and resolve D03 Doctrine-versus-Positions before implementing the remaining AI failure path; execute live Cloudinary, Resend, cache, analytics and tile outage drills once staging accounts exist.
5. Security approves or amends the implemented hashed recovery/recent-TOTP step-up, durable provider notification and WebAuthn security-key baseline; approves the authenticator/attestation/back-up policy; verifies live provider/DNS delivery; then runs physical-key custody/recovery plus supervised AMANOR-116 recovery drills and the independent authentication review.
6. Provision independent non-production web, Admin/CMS and API deployments plus MongoDB, Cloudinary, email, calendar, DNS, monitoring and tile-provider accounts; then execute staging evidence gates.
7. Convert approved items to Jira using the IDs and acceptance evidence in this ledger; keep Jira and this file synchronised.
8. Exit Sprint 0 only after architecture, scope, roles, separate build pipelines and external review bookings are approved.
9. Legal approves the editorial-audit retention schedule (AMANOR-113), then Operations rehearses the documented audited takedown and mistaken-publish recovery within 15 minutes in staging (AMANOR-145).
10. Configure Cloudflare WAF and the approved bot challenge on the deployed public routes, verify trusted proxy/client-IP handling, and execute the AMANOR-118 staging abuse drills before security review.
